APPROVED ON AUGUST 19, 2026 
BY THE BOARD OF COUNTY COMMISSIONERS 
AT THE WEEKLY BUSINESS SESSION 
LAND USE HEARING 
Monday, August 10, 2026, at 9:00 a.m. 
Anne G. Basker Auditorium, 600 NW Sixth Street, Grants Pass, OR 97526 
Present: Ron Smith, Chair; Colene Martin, Vice-Chair; and Gary Richardson, Commissioner; Nikole Guzman, 
Recorder 
Pursuant to notice through the media and in conformance with the Public Meeting Law, Ron Smith, Chair, called the 
meeting to order at 9:00 a.m. Items discussed were as follows: 
1. LAND USE HEARING: (Hearings are conducted in accordance with Josephine County Code Chapter 19.31 - Public Hearings, a copy of 
which is available from staff.) 
Request for an Appeal objecting to the Director's Decision to Deny the placement of a Non-Farm 
Dwelling on property located in the Exclusive Farm (EF) zone with access from Lower River Road; 
Assessor's Map 36-06-15-C0, Tax Lot 2800. 
Commissioner Smith read the Procedures for De Novo Appeal Land Use Hearing and explained that, because the 
Planning Director's decision was made without a public hearing, the Board would conduct a completely new, open 
evidentiary hearing. He reviewed the applicable hearing rules, the requirement that testimony and evidence address the 
relevant approval criteria, the opportunity to request a continuance or an open record, party-status provisions, and the 
order and time limits for testimony. 
Commissioner Smith asked whether anyone objected to the jurisdiction of the Board to hear the matter. None heard. 
Commissioners Richardson, Martin, and Smith each declared no conflict of interest, and no Commissioner abstained 
from the proceeding. 
Staff Report: Exhibit 1 (pages 1-129) 
Tami Smith, Josephine County Senior Planner, presented the staff report and Planning PowerPoint presentation 
concerning the Dirling Revocable Living Trust appeal. The application seeks approval for a Non-Farm Dwelling on 
approximately 2.71 acres in the Exclusive Farm zone. The application was submitted February 6, 2026, deemed 
complete March 5, 2026, and denied by the Planning Director on May 22, 2026. 
Staff explained that the denial was based primarily on the parcel being composed predominantly of Class II soils mapped 
as prime farmland if irrigated. Approximately 84 percent of the parcel is mapped as Newberg fine sandy loam, Class II, 
and approximately 16 percent as a Class IV soil. The property also has irrigation water rights. Under the applicable 
criteria, land composed predominantly of Class I through IV soils is presumed suitable for farm use, and a parcel is not 
considered generally unsuitable solely because of its size or location when it can reasonably be used in conjunction with 
other farm land. 
Staff noted that the Board, conducting a de novo review, could agree with, change, or add to the Director's findings after 
considering the evidence. If the application were approved, proposed conditions of approval were included in Exhibit 
N. Staff also identified written materials submitted after the original staff report, including August 6, 2026 materials 
from Justin Gerlitz, PE, containing Richard A. Yogi's technical soil opinion and test-pit information, and written 
opposition submitted on behalf of Lindi and Buck Baker. 
Proponents: 
a. Appellant's introductory presentation - 10 minutes 
Justin Gerlitz, PE, Gerlitz Engineering Consultants, appeared on behalf of the appellant and owner/applicant, 
Dirling Revocable Living Trust. Mr. Gerlitz stated that the dispute centered primarily on whether the parcel is 
generally unsuitable for farm use and whether it could reasonably be used in conjunction with surrounding 
agricultural land. He emphasized that the application was not based merely on whether plants could grow on 
the parcel, but on whether the land could support a viable farming operation. 
Mr. Gerlitz described the parcel as approximately 2.71 acres, with a flag-shaped access portion and a long- 
established shared driveway/easement along the west side. He stated that, after accounting for the access 
easement and the portion already mapped as non-prime soil, approximately 1.3 acres remained as the principal 
area at issue. He also described the surrounding pattern of residential and farm uses, including rural residential 
properties along the Rogue River, and stated that a no-rise engineering analysis had been completed for the 
proposed home in the floodway and that a broader approximately 2,000-acre land-use study had been completed 
as part of the application. 
Non-Farm Dwelling Appeal 
August 10, 2026 
Page [ 2 
Mr. Gerlitz discussed the additional soil information prepared by Richard A. Vogl. Ten test pits were dug across 
the subject property. According to the written analysis, a relatively shallow layer of surface soil was found over 
a dense cobble/river-rock layer. Mr. Gerlitz argued that the depth and physical limitations of the soil would 
require additional water, fertilizer, and inputs that would make commercial crop production economically 
impractical. He further relied on written statements from area farmers and neighbors who believed the parcel 
was too small or impractical to farm profitably or in conjunction with their existing operations. 
Mr. Gerlitz stated that the remaining non-farm dwelling criteria had been addressed and argued that the 
proposed dwelling would not materially alter the stability of surrounding farm uses. He explained that the 
proposed structure would be a single-family residence intended to allow the applicants' aging parents to live 
near family. 
b. Proponents (Expert) Witnesses - 5 minutes each 
No live expert witness appeared. Richard A. Vogl, MS, PG, CHG, CEG, submitted a written technical opinion 
concerning soil depth, stream-gravel deposits, agricultural productivity, and the economic feasibility of farming 
the parcel. Caleb Hunter submitted a written statement regarding farming experience and the practical and 
economic feasibility of farming the property. 
c. Others in Favor - 5 minutes each 
Written support in the record included statements from Luis S. Hernandez, Emily Bertman, and Gabrielle Fey. 
The appellant summarized those materials as supporting the position that the parcel is impractical to farm 
commercially because of its limited size, soil conditions, access, and surrounding development. 
d. Questions of Staff/ Appellant ( directed through the Chairperson) - 5 minutes 
Commissioner Richardson asked about the location of the ten test pits, the qualifications of the geologist, 
whether laboratory testing had been performed, whether the irrigation water right had been exercised, and 
whether the appellant's position concerned physical impossibility or economic feasibility. Mr. Gerlitz responded 
that the test pits were distributed across the parcel; no laboratory analysis was performed; the water right had 
been used; and the report focused on whether the site could economically support a farming operation given the 
shallow soils over dense cobble. 
Commissioner Martin asked about the proposed structure, whether other structures existed on the subject parcel, 
the surrounding residences, and the purpose of the requested dwelling. Mr. Gerlitz stated that no other structure 
currently exists on the subject parcel and that the proposal is for one single-family home. Commissioner Smith 
asked about the zoning and soil classifications of nearby residential properties and noted the presence of 
residences on land in the surrounding Exclusive Farm area. 
Opponents: 
a. Representative - l O minutes 
James R. Dole, Watkinson Laird Rubenstein PC, appeared on behalf of adjacent landowners Lindi and Buck 
Baker. Mr. Dole argued that the appellant bears the burden of proving compliance with the applicable criteria 
by substantial evidence and that the record did not establish that the parcel is generally unsuitable for farm use. 
He questioned the sufficiency of the written farmer statements and argued that surrounding active farm uses 
demonstrate the agricultural character of the area. 
Mr. Dole further argued that the existence of the shared asphalt driveway does not make the land unsuitable for 
agriculture because farm equipment routinely crosses paved driveways and public roads. He also expressed 
concern that approval of an additional non-farm dwelling would contribute to residential encroachment into the 
Exclusive Farm area and could affect the long-term stability of agricultural use. 
b. Opposition (Expert) Witnesses - 5 minutes each 
None heard. 
c. Others in Opposition - 5 minutes each 
Buck Baker, a longtime Josephine County resident and adjacent landowner, spoke in opposition. He described 
the agricultural character and fertile river soils of the area, his own experience maintaining and using land on 
both sides of an asphalt driveway, and surrounding farming operations. He stated that his property has 
productive grass, gardens, and other agricultural use and argued that the subject parcel likewise contains high- 
value farm soil. He also raised concerns regarding increasing residential development in the Exclusive Farm 
zone and asked the Board to uphold the Planning Director's denial. 
Lindi Baker, an adjacent landowner, also spoke in opposition. She stated that agricultural operations exist on 
nearby parcels and that she had personally observed the subject parcel producing alfalfa in earlier years. She 
argued that parcel size alone is not sufficient to establish general unsuitability because smaller acreage may be 
leased or used in conjunction with other farm operations. She requested that the Board follow the existing soil 
Non-Farm Dwelling Appeal 
August IO, 2026 
Page I 3 
mapping and deny the appeal. 
d. Questions of Staff (directed through the Chairperson) - 5 minutes 
No separate questions were presented during the opponents' designated question period. 
Staff and/or Agency Comments: 
James Black, Josephine County Planning Manager/Director, addressed the additional soil report. He explained 
that Oregon law establishes a specific process for challenging or changing mapped agricultural soil 
classifications and that the state maintains a limited list of qualified soil professionals authorized for that work. 
Mr. Black stated that the professional who prepared the appellant's supplemental report was not on that state 
list. Accordingly, the report could not, by itself, be used to change the mapped soil classification. He stated that 
if a properly qualified soil professional completed the required evaluation and the resulting evidence supported 
a different classification, staff could consider that evidence in reviewing the application. 
In response to questions from the Board, Mr. Black stated that nearby residences may have been established 
under earlier land-use rules or other approval paths and that the fact that residences exist on surrounding EF- 
zoned properties does not eliminate the current statutory requirements applicable to this application. 
Applicant's Oral Rebuttal- JO minutes: 
Mr. Gerlitz responded that the appellant was not asking the Board to rezone the property or formally reclassify 
the soil. Rather, he argued that the non-farm dwelling criteria allow the Board to determine whether the parcel 
is generally unsuitable for farming based on the actual site conditions. He emphasized the property's limited 
usable acreage, its floodway setting, shallow topsoil over cobble, established residential uses on several sides, 
and the traffic associated with the shared driveway. He reiterated that the broader land-use criteria had been 
met and requested approval of the application. 
Opponents' Oral Surrehuttal: 
Mr. Dole responded that the supplemental geologic evidence did not satisfy the statutory requirements 
governing soil determinations and therefore should not be relied upon to overcome the existing mapped 
classification. He reiterated that small parcel size alone does not establish unsuitability and that the record did 
not contain substantial evidence showing the property could not be used in conjunction with nearby agricultural 
operations. 
Summation: 
a. Appellant or Representative - 5 minutes 
Mr. Gerlitz summarized that the appellant was not asserting that no farm soil exists on the parcel, but that the 
parcel is generally unsuitable for productive commercial farming because of the limited acreage and site- 
specific soil limitations. He again requested approval of the appeal. 
Mike Dirling addressed the Board and stated that the property had been owned by the family since 
approximately 2000. He described multiple attempts over the years to find farmers willing to use the property, 
including discussions involving vineyards and neighboring operations, but said the interested parties ultimately 
concluded that farming the parcel would not be economically worthwhile. He stated that the family is not 
seeking a larger development and only wants a residence that would allow family members to live nearby. 
b. Opponents' Representative - 5 minutes 
Mr. Dole summarized that the appellant had not met the burden of proof under the applicable criteria and again 
requested that the Planning Director's denial be upheld. Buck Baker added that, if he were commercially farming 
his adjacent land, the subject acreage would be accessible and could be used with his property, which he 
believed demonstrated that the parcel is not isolated from agricultural use. 
Discussion: 
Commissioner Richardson asked Planning staff to clarify when the parcel was created and whether the 
applicable lot-creation date requirements had been met. Staff explained that the property involved two existing 
legal lots and that a property line adjustment moved the boundary between them; the adjustment did not create 
a new lot. Commissioner Martin confirmed with staff that there had historically been two lots. 
Commissioner Richardson stated that, although he might personally prefer a different result, the Board was 
required to apply the law and the evidence in the record. He expressed concern that the required state-approved 
soil determination had not been provided and that, absent such evidence, the currently mapped soil classification 
remained controlling. Commissioner Smith focused on the surrounding residential uses, the applicants' stated 
history of unsuccessful attempts to have the land farmed, and the Board's role in the land-use appeal process. 
Commissioner Martin requested legal guidance before the Board proceeded to a decision. 
Chase Seguin, counsel for the County's Land Use Department, advised that the Board was at a procedural point 
Non-Farm Dwelling Appeal 
August 10, 2026 
Page 14 
where it could deny the appeal based on the present record or continue the hearing to allow the appellant an 
opportunity to obtain and submit the required soil documentation. Counsel explained that the applicant could 
offer additional days to extend the land-use decision deadline if more time were required, and that a continuance 
would keep the matter open for additional evidence. 
Request to Continue I Record Remains Open: 
Justin Gerlitz requested an additional seven days to address the soil-evidence issue identified during the hearing. 
The Board agreed to continue the hearing to Monday, August 17, 2026, at 10:00 a.m. The evidentiary record 
remained open, and the Board did not conduct final deliberations or make a decision on the appeal at this 
meeting. 
Commissioner Martin made a motion to continue the Land Use Hearing for seven days to Monday, August 17, 
2026, at 10:00 a.m. Commissioner Richardson seconded the motion. Upon roll call: Commissioner Richardson 
- Yes; Commissioner Martin - Yes; Commissioner Smith - Yes. Motion carried 3-0. 
Hearing Continued: 
Chair Smith continued the Land Use Hearing to Monday, August 17, 2026, at 10:00 a.m. and adjourned the August 10, 
2026 proceeding at 10:28 a.m.

APPEAL BACKGROUND 
The application was submitted on February 6, 2026 and deemed complete on March 5, 
2026 (Exhibit B). Notice of Land Use Request was mailed on March 17, 2026 (Exhibit 
C). 
This application is subject to Section 19.64.060 - General Criteria for New Dwellings; 
Section 19.64.070.A- Standards and Criteria for Non-Farm Dwellings; Section 19.64.080 
- Siting Standards; Section 19.69A.060 - Administration in floodway areas; Chapter 
19.76 - Wildfire & Emergency Safety Standards; Chapter 19.83.020 - Application of 
Standards (Erosion Control). The Director issued a decision on May 22, 2026 denying the 
proposed Non-Farm Dwelling on the basis that the subject parcel is composed 
predominantly of Class II soils and mapped as prime soils if irrigated therefore cannot be 
considered generally unsuitable for farm use (Exhibit 0).

REVIEW 
The Board of County Commissioners (hereafter BCC) must determine if the application 
complies with the standards and criteria listed below in order to determine a Non-Farm 
Dwelling approval. 
Given this hearing is de novo (new), the Board now has the task to decide if the 
application meets the standards and criteria. The Board may agree, disagree, or alter the 
findings as needed after receiving testimony during the hearing. The Board may change, 
NON-FARM DWELLING/ APPEAL 
PAGE 2 
add, and delete any conditions listed in the decision if the application is approved. If the 
application is denied, the Board must make clear findings as such. 
During the review of the application, staff evaluated the proposal for the placement of a 
non-farm dwelling under the applicable Standards and Criteria. Based on that review, the 
application was denied. The denial was issued because the majority of the subject 
property consists of predominantly Class II soils. These soils are identified by the National 
Resources Conservation Service (NRCS) as prime farmland soils Exhibit F, and are also 
classified as prime farmland if irrigated under Josephine County soil mapping Exhibit G. 
In addition, the property has existing irrigation water rights as documented by the Oregon 
Water Resources Department (OWRD) Exhibit H. 
The appellant's application challenges the Director's findings. (Exhibit A) Staff mailed out 
Notice of De Novo Evidentiary Appeal Hearing. Exhibit I 
Staff has included additional comments received from the neighbors in opposition 
(Exhibit J). and additional comments from neighbors in support (Exhibit K-M). 
NOTE: If the Board approves the Non-Farm Dwelling application, staff provides conditions 
of approval for consideration in Exhibit N. 
ST AND ARDS & CRITERIA (for reference) 
Section 19.64.060 - General Requirements for New Dwellings 
A. 
The proposed non-farm dwelling will not force a significant change in or 
significantly increase the cost of accepted farm or forest practices on 
surrounding lands devoted to farm or forest use; 
Finding: Based on the surrounding development pattern, there appears to 
be minimal active farm or forest use in the immediate vicinity. The subject 
parcel is bordered by existing rural residential development on three sides, 
with the Rogue River located to the south. The development does not 
appear to adversely affect or increase the cost of accepted farm or forest 
practices on surrounding resource lands. 
B. 
As condition of approval, the landowner for the dwelling shall sign and 
record in the county deed records a document binding the landowner, and 
the landowner's successors in interest, prohibiting them from pursuing claim 
for relief or cause of action alleging injury from farming or forest practices 
for which no action or claim is allowed under ORS 30.936 or 30.937. 
Finding: If application is approved, the applicant shall submit a signed, 
notarized, and recorded Waiver of Remonstrance Regarding Dwellings in 
Farm Use. 
NON-FARM DWELLING/APPEAL 
PAGE 3 
Section 19.64.070.A- Standards and Criteria for Non-Farm Dwellings 
A. 
Dwellings Not in Conjunction with Farm Use (Non-Farm Dwellings). 
One single-family residential dwelling not provided in conjunction with 
commercial farm use, based on findings demonstrating that all of the 
following requirements are met: 
1. 
The dwelling is situated upon a lot or parcel, or a portion of a lot or parcel, 
which is generally unsuitable land for the production of farm crops and 
livestock or merchantable tree species, considering the terrain, adverse soil 
or land conditions, drainage and flooding, vegetation, location and size of 
the tract. 
a. 
A lot or parcel or portion of a lot or parcel shall not be considered 
unsuitable solely because of size or location if it can reasonably be 
put to farm or forest use in conjunction with other land; and 
b. 
A lot or parcel or portion of a lot or parcel is not "generally 
unsuitable" simply because it is too small to be farmed 
profitably by itself. If a lot or parcel or portion of a lot or parcel 
can be sold, leased, rented or otherwise managed as a part of a 
commercial farm or ranch, then the lot or parcel or portion of 
the lot or parcel is not "generally unsuitable." A lot or parcel or 
portion of a lot or parcel is presumed to be suitable if it is 
composed predominantly of Class I-IV soils. Just because a lot 
or parcel or portion of a lot or parcel is unsuitable for one farm 
use does not mean it is not suitable for another farm use; or 
c. 
If the parcel is under forest assessment, the dwelling shall be 
situated upon generally unsuitable land for the production of 
merchantable tree species recognized by the forest practices rules, 
considering the terrain, adverse soil or land conditions, drainage 
and flooding, vegetation, location and size of the parcel. In addition: 
[1] If a lot or parcel is under forest assessment, the area is not 
"generally unsuitable" simply because it is too small to be 
managed for forest production profitably by itself. If a lot or 
parcel under forest assessment can be sold, leased, rented or 
otherwise managed as a part of a forestry operation, it is not 
"generally unsuitable." 
[2] If a lot or parcel is under forest assessment, it is presumed 
suitable if it is composed predominantly of soils capable of 
producing 50 cubic feet of wood fiber per acre per year; 
NON-FARM DWELLING/APPEAL 
PAGE 4 
d. 
If a lot or parcel is under forest assessment, a finding must be made 
that the dwelling is compatible with and will not seriously interfere 
with forest uses on surrounding lands and will not force a significant 
change in forest practices or significantly increase the cost of those 
practices on the surrounding lands. 
Finding: The subject property is approximately 2.71 acres in size and 
contains two mapped soil types: Camas gravelly sandy loam (14), 
comprising approximately 16 percent of the site and rated as Class IV if 
irrigated and non-irrigated, and Newberg fine sandy loam (57), comprising 
approximately 84 percent of the site and rated as Class II if irrigated and non- 
irrigated. Because the parcel is composed predominantly of Class I-IV soils, 
the parcel is presumed to be suitable for farm use pursuant to the applicable 
code criteria. 
The proposed homesite is located within the portion of the property mapped 
as Newberg fine sandy loam, which is rated as Class II soil and is generally 
well suited for irrigated hay and pasture production. In addition, the 
Josephine County soil mapping indicates that the majority of the property 
consists of prime soils if irrigated, and the property has water rights through 
the Fort Vannoy Irrigation District under Certificate 85233. 
Although the parcel is physically separated from adjacent resource lands by 
existing development features, including a shared asphalt driveway to the 
west serving multiple residences and an irrigation canal to the north, 
Planning could not determine that the parcel or portion of the parcel cannot 
reasonably be sold, leased, rented, or otherwise managed as part of a 
commercial farm or ranch operation due to the predominance of Class I-IV 
soils and the agricultural capability classifications of the property. 
According to the Josephine County Assessor's records, the property is not 
currently under forest assessment; therefore, 
the provisions of Section 
19.64.070(A)(1)(c) and (d), JGC, do not apply. 
2. 
The dwelling will not materially alter the stability of the overall land use 
pattern on the area subject to OAR 660-033-0130(4)(c)(C). 
Finding: The applicant submitted a cumulative impacts and land use pattern 
analysis to evaluate whether the proposed nonfarm dwelling would materially alter 
the stability of the surrounding land use pattern, as required by OAR 660-033- 
0130(4)(c)(C). The study area encompasses approximately 2,036.91 acres of 
adjacent and nearby properties and includes 183 Exclusive Farm (EF) zoned tax 
lots. Within the study area, 126 tax lots contain existing dwellings and 57 tax lots 
do not contain dwellings. Of the 126 developed lots, 87 were identified as 
containing farm dwellings and 39 were identified as containing nonfarm dwellings. 
Farm versus nonfarm dwelling status was evaluated using available Josephine 
NON-FARM DWELLING/APPEAL 
PAGE 5 
County Assessor records and corroborated with aerial and historical imagery 
where necessary. The analysis demonstrates that approximately 68 percent of the 
parcels within the study area contain dwellings, with an average parcel size of 
approximately 11. 13 acres. This reflects an established pattern of rural residential 
development within the EF zone rather than large contiguous blocks devoted 
exclusively to commercial agricultural use. The applicant also evaluated the 57 
undeveloped parcels within the study area to determine their potential eligibility for 
nonfarm dwellings. The evaluation considered predominant soil classifications, 
whether the parcels could reasonably be managed in conjunction with adjacent 
farm operations, the character of surrounding land uses, and the existing 
development pattern in the vicinity. Based on those factors, the analysis concluded 
that approximately six parcels within the study area may potentially qualify for 
nonfarm dwellings. Based on the evidence submitted, the proposed dwelling does 
not appear likely to materially alter the stability of the overall land use pattern within 
the surrounding area subject to OAR 660-033-0130(4)(c)(C). 
3. 
The dwelling will be sited on a lot or parcel created before January 1, 1993, 
or on a lot or parcel created after January 1, 1993, when the lot or parcel was 
created in conformance with the requirements of Section JCC 19.64.090.C. 
Finding: Planning and Assessor records indicate that the subject parcel (Tax Lot 
2800) was originally created by Warranty Deed (V327 P786) on October 27, 1977. 
The parcel was subsequently reconfigured through Partition Plat 2025-31 on 
September 16, 2025, resulting in its current size of 2. 71 acres. Because the parcel 
was originally created prior to January 1, 1993, and the subsequent reconfiguration 
was completed in conformance with applicable County land division requirements, 
4. 
A non-farm dwelling may not be approved on a lot or parcel that is already 
established with a single-family dwelling is established on a lot or parcel as 
a lot of record dwelling per subsection 19.64.070.B, JCC. 
Finding: Planning and Assessor records indicate that the parcel is currently 
vacant, and no other dwelling exists on the property. 
5. 
Applicant shall submit proof that the lot or parcel has been disqualified for 
valuation for farm use under ORS 308A. 050 to 308A.128 or other special 
assessment under ORS 308A.315, 321.257 to 321.390, 321.700 to 321.754 
or 321.805 to 321.855. and any additional tax imposed as the result of 
disqualification has been paid pursuant to the requirement of ORS 215.236. 
Finding: Per the Josephine County Assessor records the subject property is not 
receiving special farm assessment. 
Section 19.64.080 - Siting Standards 
A. 
The dwelling or activities associated with the dwelling will not force a

significant change in or significantly increase the cost of accepted farming 
or forest practices on nearby lands devoted to farm or forest use; 
Finding: If approved it does not appear that the proposed development would

B. 
The placement of dwellings shall be located on the least productive, 
buildable portion of the parcel taking into consideration terrain, adverse soil 
or land conditions, drainage and flooding, access, vegetation, location and

Finding: The applicant has submitted a no-rise certification prepared by a

C. 
A lot or parcel shall not be considered unsuitable solely because of size or

Finding: The subject parcel is not considered unsuitable solely because of its size 
or location. Based on the mapped soil classifications, including the presence of

The dwelling will not materially alter the stability of the overall land use

Finding: As discussed above, the dwelling does not appear to materially alter the 
stability of the overall land use pattern in the area. 
F. 
If the dwelling is established under JCC 19.64.070.A or B, then additional 
dwellings may not be approved or sited. 
Finding: No additional dwellings were proposed.

Section 19.69A.060 -Administration in floodway areas 
The property contains the floodplain of the Rogue River. The proposed home-site 
is entirely within the floodway. The structure shall be located at the site which is 
least impacted by inundation. The dwelling shall meet all development permit 
requirements of Section 19.69A.080 JGC and all property development standards 
of Section 19.69A.090 JGC. 
Finding: The applicant submitted a No-Rise Flood Analysis prepared by the Galli 
Group, which appears to demonstrate that the proposed development will not 
result in an increase in base flood elevation within the floodway. However, if the 
application is approved, the applicant shall submit an application for a Technical 
Plan Review to the Planning Department for floodplain review and verification of 
compliance with Sections 19. 69A. 080 and 19. 69A. 090, JGC. Any change in 
homesite location requires a new study. 
Section 19.76.020.D - Wildfire and Emergency Safety Standards 
New dwellings in any zone other than the forest zone shall demonstrate 
compliance with all of the applicable standards of Chapter 19. 76 JCC. 
Finding: If approved, 
the applicant shall submit a wildfire safety plan 
demonstrating compliance with the requirements of Chapter 19. 76, JGC prior to 
final development permit. 
Section 19.83.020 - Application of Standards (Erosion Control) 
These standards apply to any land use application including development and 
construction which would require any grading or filling on slopes that are 15% or 
greater or soils that are granitic in composition as mapped by the NRCS except 
when authorized or regulated by the Oregon Forest Practices Act. 
Finding: The property contains granitic soils. If approved, the applicant shall 
submit an erosion and sediment control plan prior to issuance of final development 
permit. 
HEARING FORMAT 
The appeal is subject to the rules and procedures found in Chapter 19.33 of the Josephine 
County Code (JCC). All appeals of a Director's decision shall be heard by the Board as a 
de novo hearing (a fully, open evidentiary hearing). The Board may affirm, reverse or 
modify a decision on appeal or send the decision back to the Director for additional 
consideration or action.

The order of procedure for new hearings requires the appeal applicant (appellant) to go 
first and present the application. The following is a general outline of the order of 
procedure: 
1. 
Planning Staff Report 
2. 
Appellant's Case (Gerlitz/Dirling) 
3. 
Public Testimony/Parties in favor or against. 
4. 
Rebuttal by Appellant (Gerlitz/Dirling) 
5. 
Surrebuttal - (discretionary) 
6. 
Summation by Appellant (Gerlitz/Dirling) 
7. 
Summation by Opponent's representative (if any) 
8. 
Questions by Board to parties and/or staff 
9. 
Close of Public Hearing 
10. 
Deliberations 
A more detailed description of the order of procedure for public hearings is contained in 
Section 19.31.120 JCC.

CONSULTANTS 
Lower River Road Non-Farm Dwelling-Appeal of Land Use Decision 
The Dirling Revocable Living Trust (applicant) respectfully appeals the Planning Director's 
denial of the proposed Non-Farm Dwelling application. 
The Applicant requests that the Board of County Commissioners reverse the Planning Director's 
decision and approve the application as proposed. The appeal is based on the Director's finding 
that the subject parcel is not generally unsuitable for farm use because mapped soil 
classifications indicate the presence of Class II and other agriculturally capable soils. 
The Applicant does not dispute that portions of the property contain mapped agricultural soils. 
However, the applicable standard does not end with soil classification. The standard requires 
evaluation of the parcel's actual suitability for farm use considering terrain, adverse soil or land 
conditions, drainage and flooding, vegetation, location, size, and whether the parcel can 
reasonably be used in conjunction with other agricultural land. The County's decision gives 
controlling weight to mapped soils while failing to adequately consider the actual physical and 
land use constraints that make this parcel generally unsuitable for farm use. 
Basis for Appeal 
A. The decision improperlv treats mapped soils as determinative. 
The Planning Director's denial is based primarily on the presence of mapped Class II and 
other agriculturally capable soils. However, the applicable standard requires a broader 
analysis than soil mapping alone. 
OAR 660-033-0130 requires the County to evaluate whether the dwelling is situated on 
land that is generallv unsuitable for farm crops, livestock, or merchantable tree species 
by considering "terrain, adverse soil or land conditions, drainage and flooding, 
vegetation, location and size of the tract." 
The decision acknowledges the mapped soils but does not adequately evaluate the 
parcel's functional limitations. The parcel is 2.71 acres in gross area, but approximately 
1.27 acres are occupied by the flagpole/access corridor and 50-foot-wide shared access 
easement. This leaves approximately 1.43 acres of actual usable property, Of that usable 
area, only approximately 1.30 acres are mapped as farm soils (see Exhibit "A"). 
The practical effect is that the County evaluated the parcel as though it were a functional 
2. 71-acre farm tract, when the actual farmable area is closer to 1.30 acres and is 
constrained by a narrow configuration, surrounding residential uses, and a permanent 
residential access corridor. 
The denial should be reversed because the County's conclusion is based on the presence 
of mapped soils rather than the parcel's actual ability to support farm use. 
223 NE "B" Street, Grants Pass, OR 97526 
Office: 541-244-2617 
www.gerlitzengineering.com 
Exhibit A - 7 
Page 1 
B. The parcel cannot reasonably be farmed in conjunction with adjacent agricultural land. 
The key question is not whether agricultural soils exist somewhere on the property, but 
whether this parcel can reasonably be put to farm use by itself or in conjunction with 
other land. 
The only farrnable agricultural land in the immediate vicinity is located to the west. 
However, the subject property is separated from that farmland by the existing asphalt 
driveway and so-foot-wide shared access easement. The original application specifically 
identified the shared asphalt driveway along the west property line, located within the 
access easement, which provides access to multiple existing single-family 
residences/parcels along the riverfront. 
That access corridor is not temporary, speculative, or likely to disappear. Tbe 
driveway/easement serves five existing Rural Residential properties, three of which are 
developed with longstanding single-family homes (see Exhibit "B"). In addition, due to a 
somewhat recent fire, there are two additional Rural Residential lots that will be 
developed with new homes in the near future. Because those properties are residential 
and not EF-zoned farm parcels, the access easement will remain a permanent residential 
access feature (see Exhibit "C"). In addition to these five residential parcels, there is a 
sixth EF-zoned parcel with a single-family home that relies on the shared driveway for 
access. 
This access corridor cannot legally be absorbed into a farm operation or treated as 
available agricultural land. Additionally, it is not reasonable to assume an adjacent 
farming operation would continually cross this access corridor with heavy farming 
equipment to farm an additional i.go-acres. After considering the costs for irrigation, 
farm equipment, sales charges, and ongoing maintenance of the asphalt access corridor 
due to repeated heavy equipment crossings, the cost of farm production on such a small 
parcel would far outweigh any profits received. 
After removing the access easement, the remaining usable portion of the subject 
property is only approximately 120 feet wide. That narrow remnant is not a practical 
agricultural unit and is not reasonably combinable with the western farmland across the 
paved residential access corridor. 
The application materials already explained that the asphalt driveway physically 
separates the subject parcel from western farmland and prevents the parcel from being 
effectively managed as part of a commercial farm unit. The County's denial does not 
adequately rebut that evidence. Instead, it relies on mapped soils to conclude that the 
parcel appears capable of being used with other land. That conclusion is not supported 
by the actual site configuration. 
C. The Director's own findings confirm that the remaining approval criteria are satisfied. 
The appeal should be narrowly focused because the Director's decision appears to 
acknowledge that the other major approval criteria are satisfied. 
The decision states that, if approved, the proposed development would not appear to 
materially interfere with accepted farm or forest practices in the area. It also 
223 NE "B" Street, Grants Pass, OR 97526 
Office: 541-244-2617 
www.gerlitzengineering.com 
Exhibit A - 8 
Page 2 
acknowledges that the Applicant submitted a no-rise certification prepared by a 
registered engineer, that the parcel is vacant with no dwelling on the property, that the 
parcel would satisfy the applicable creation-date requirements, and that the dwelling 
does not appear likely to materially alter the stability of the overall land use pattern. 
Those findings are significant. They show that the denial is not based on impacts to 
surrounding farming, instability of the land use pattern, floodplain noncompliance, 
parcel legality, or the existence of another dwelling on the parcel. The denial turns on 
one narrow issue which is whether the property is generally unsuitable for farm use. 
When the parcel is evaluated based on its actual conditions rather than gross acreage 
and GIS soil mapping, the evidence supports the finding that it is generally unsuitable 
for farm use. The parcel has only approximately 1.43 acres of usable area after 
accounting for the access corridor, only approximately 1.30 acres of mapped farm soils 
within that usable area, a remaining width of approximately 120 feet, residential 
development to the north, east, and south, and a permanent asphalt driveway /access 
easement serving riverfront residential properties that separates it from the farmland to 
the west. 
Conclusion 
The Applicant firmly believes that the denial relies too heavily on mapped soil classifications and 
does not adequately account for the actual physical and land use constraints that determine 
whether this parcel can realistically function as farmland. The Director's findings clearly 
indicate that the proposal would not materially interfere with accepted farm practices and would 
not materially alter the stability of the land use pattern, both of which are significant in any non- 
farm dwelling approval. 
For these reasons, the Applicant respectfully requests that the Board of County Commissioners 
reverse the Planning Director's denial and approve the Non-Farm Dwelling application. 
223 NE "B" Street, Grants Pass, OR 97526 
Office: 541-244-2617

Non-Farm Dwelling Application Narrative 
Attached are the formal application submittal documents for a proposed non-farm dwelling in 
the Exclusive Farm Use (EFU) zone. The project is located on Lower River Road, in Grants Pass 
(36-06-15-Co, Tax Lot 2800). 
A recent property line adjustment revised the parcel area to 2.71-acres, and a recent no-rise 
analysis was conducted to comply with the standards for building in the FEMA floodway. 
Existing Conditions 
The 2.71-acre parcel is relatively flat and consists primarily of grass cover, with a row of trees 
along the east properly line. The predominant soil type on site is Newberg Fine Sandy Loam, 
which is classified as a Class 3 soil type and is considered prime farmland soil. However, the 
northern portion of the property near the proposed homesite consists of Camas Gravelly Sandy 
Loam, which is not considered prime soil (see Exhibit "A" & "B"). 
A shared asphalt driveway, located within a so-foot-wide shared access easement, runs along 
the west property line and provides access to three existing single-family homes in addition to 
the proposed residence. A concrete irrigation canal borders the north property line, and an 
existing groundwater well is located on site (sec Exhibit "A"). 
The parcels directly adjacent to the north, east, and south contain residential dwellings and are 
not currently being used for farm use. 
Proposed Conditions 
The proposed development will add a single-family residence to the parcel. The project will 
utilize the existing asphalt driveway and on-site groundwater well. A new septic system will be 
installed to serve the home. 
Carrying Capacity 
Water System 
An existing groundwater well exists on-site and will be utilized for the new dwelling (see Exhibit 
"F") 
• Well 1.D. JOSE 62104 - 36-06-15-Co, TL 2800 
o Air Test - 
22.5 GPM 
22.5 GPM is well above the required flow needed for a residential home proving adequate 
carrying capacity to support the development. 
223 NE "B" Street, Grants Pass, OR 97526 
Office: 541-244-2617 
www.gerlitzengineering.com 
Exhibit A - 16 
Page 1 
Septic Svstem 
A recent septic site evaluation from the Department of Environmental Quality was conducted 
for the dwelling (see Exhibit "G"). Per the evaluation, a standard septic system can adequately 
support the proposed dwelling. 
FEMA No-Rise Analysis 
A HEC-RAS analysis was conducted to verify that a no-rise condition will exist after the 
structure is constructed. In addition to confirming no-rise conditions, the report establishes 
minimum finish-floor elevations for the construction of the building (see Exhibit "H''). 
Non-Farm Dwelling Standards and Criteria 
The EF Zone permits the proposed homesite pursuant to Section 19.64.070. In addition to the 
standards and criteria in Section 19.64.070, this application must also demonstrate compliance

General Criteria for New Dwellings - Section 19.64.060 JCC 
19.64.060 JCC requires new dwellings to be reviewed against, and comply with, the following 
standards and/or criteria: 
A. The use or activities associated with the use will not force significant change in or 
significantly increase the cost of accepted farm or forest practices on surrounding land 
devoted to farm or forest use; 
Response: The proposed single-family dwelling will not force a significant change in or 
result in a significant increase in the cost of 
accepted farming or forest practices on 
surrounding land devoted to farm or forest use. 
The subject parcel is bordered by existing rural residential development on three sides 
which has already established a long-standing residential presence in the immediate 
area. In addition, the area beyond the immediately adjacent parcel to the east consists 
of further residential development, including multiple long-term trailer parks, rather 
than contiguous or actively managedfarmland. These existing land use patterns 
demonstrate that surrounding farm and forest operators have already adjusted their 
practices to the presence of 
nearby rural residences and the addition of 
one more 
dwelling will not constitute a significant change. 
1'o the south, the adjacent parcel is bounded by the river, and residential development 
continues across the river as well. This condition provides both physical separation and 
a clear residential land use context in that direction, substantially reducing the 
potential for conflicts with agricultural or forestry operations and limiting any 
potential impacts related to noise, dust, odors, spray drift, or operational timing. The 
river corridor and residential development pattern effectively isolate the subject parcel 
from large contiguous tracts of 
farm or forest land where operational constraints could 
be triggered by new development.

Standards and Criteria for Non-Farm Dwellings - 19.64.070 
There are three ways to permit a Non-Farm Dwelling in the EF Zone: 
A. Dwellings Not in Conjunction with Farm Use 
B. Lot of Records Dwellings 
C. High Value Farmland- 
Option Number 1 and Option Number 2

dwelling in the EF Zone.

a. A lot or parcel or portion of a lot or parcel shall not be considered unsuitable

a part of a commercial farm or ranch, then the lot or parcel or portion of the 
lot or parcel is not "generally unsuitable." A lot or parcel or portion of a lot or

property line, which serves three residetuial homes beyond the subject parcel. 
This driveway functions as a developed access corridor that prevents practical 
movement of farm equipment across it and limits the parcel's ability to be 
managed as part of 
the adjacent farm unit. In addition, an irrigation canal 
along the northern property line separates the subject parcel.from the parcel to 
the north.further limiting any potentialfor integration with nearby resource 
lands. 
The parcel is also surrounded by existing residential development to the north, 
south, and east, with additional residential zoning and development in the 
vicinity and across the Rogue River. This setting places the parcel within a 
residential corridor rather than within a contiguous agricultural block, and it 
prevents the parcel from being reasonably sold, leased, rented, or otherwise 
managed as part of 
a commercial farm operation. Consequently, the parcel's 
unsuitability is based on the inability to reasonably use it in conjunction with 
other farmlands, not simply its size or location. 
4. If the parcel is under forest assessment, the dwelling shall be sited upon generally 
unsuitable land for the production of merchantable tree species recognized under 
the forest practice rules considering the terrain, adverse soil or land conditions, 
drainage and flooding, vegetation, location and size of the parcel; 
Response: Not Applicable. The subject parcel is not under forest assessment. 
5. The dwelling will not materially alter the stability of the overall land use pattern 
of the area; and 
Response: The proposed dwelling will not materially alter the stability of 
the 
overall land use pattern of 
the area. The subject parcel is already located within 
an established residential corridor, with existing residential development 
immediately to the north, south, and east, additional residential development 
and trailer parks nearby, and residential development across the Rogue River. 
In this context, the dwelling will be consistent with the surrounding pattern of 
scattered residences associated with existing parcelization and will not 
introduce a new or incompatible land use trend within the area. 
The dwelling will be accessed using the existing shared asphalt driueuxu) along 
the western property line, which alreadii serves multiple residences and reflects 
an established residential access pattern. Because the surrounding landscape 
already includes multiple dwellings and residential uses interspersed within the 
farm zone, the proposal will not create a destabilizing precedent or 
incrementally shift the area toward a new land use pattern. The cumulative 
impacts analysis prepared for this application further demonstrates that the 
223 NE "B" Street, Grants Pass, OR 97526 
Office: 541-244-2617 
www.gerlitzengineering.com 
Exhibit A - 28 
Page 13 
proposed dwelling will not contribute to a level of 
nonfarm development that 
would materially alter the overall land use pattern of 
the study area. 
6. If the dwelling is established under JCC 19.64.ozo(A) or (B), then additional 
dwellings may not be approved or sited. [2005 RLDC § 64.080.] 
Response: This criterion is noted. If the dwelling is established under JCC 
19.64.07o(A) or (B), then additional dwellings may not be approved or sited on 
the parcel, consistent with JCC 19.64.080. 
Property Development Standards - Section 19.64.095 JCC 
The proposal will comply with all listed permit review requirements and property development 
standards listed in this section that are applicable to the project. This includes but is not limited 
to required setbacks, access, erosion control plans/measures, stream/river setbacks, 
development within the mapped floodway, and water standards. 
Summary 
In summary, the proposed nonfarm dwelling on Lower River Road satisfies the applicable 
approval criteria. The subject parcel is generally unsuitable for farm use due to its location 
within an established residential corridor, physical barriers that prevent reasonable integration 
with adjacent farm land, and significant constraints associated with its FEMA floodway 
designation. The dwelling is sited 011 the least productive, buildable portion of the parcel to 
minimize impacts to prime soils and to address flooding and access limitations, and it will not 
force changes to accepted farming practices or materially alter the stability of the overall land 
use pattern. The required cumulative impacts analysis further supports these findings, and the 
limitation on additional dwellings is acknowledged as a continuing restriction.

THIS IS NOT YOUR PERMIT. A Construction/Installation permit is required before you construct your system. Please contact 
this office when you are ready to apply for a construction/installation permit. We cannot sign off on any Building Codes forms 
until we issue your permit 
This site approval runs with the land and will automatically benefit subsequent owners. This site approval is valid until the 
approved system is constructed under a construction permit or unless the site is altered without approval from this office. 
Alterations/excavations/lot line adjustments made to the site, or placement of wells or utilities, etc., may invalidate this approval 
If you disagree with the decision of this report, you may apply for a site evaluation report review. The application for a site 
evaluation report review must be submitted to DEQ in writing within 60 days after the site evaluation report Issue date and must 
include the site evaluation review fee in OAR 34~071-0140 Table 9A. A senior DEQ staff person will be assigned the site 
evaluation report review application. 
You may apply for a variance to the onsite wastewater treatment system rules. The variance application must include a copy of 
the site evaluation report, plans and specifications for the proposed system, specify the rule(s) to which a variance is being 
requested, demonstrate the variance is warranted, and include the variance fee in OAR 340-071-140 Table 9C. A variance may 
only be granted if the variance officer determines that strict compliance with a rule is inappropriate or special physical conditions 
render strict compliance unreasonable, burdensome or impractical. A senior DEQ variance officer will be assigned the variance 
application.

Planning Staff: 
This is to certify that my site evaluation and Simple Floodway Encroachment Analysis, 
as presented in the Rogue River No-Rise Flood Analysis, New Residential Development 
report, dated September 22, 2025, attached, verifies that the proposed construction of the 
subject residential development project on the subject site located at T36S, R06W, 
SEC l 5C, TL 2800 on Lower River Road in Grants Pass, Oregon, results in a No-Rise 
Declaration. 
According to the Simple Floodway Encroachment Analysis results, the proposed new 
development, including the mitigation area, will result in no loss of flow conveyance and 
will not adversely affect the I 00-year Rogue River flood elevations, or Floodway widths 
and elevations, on this or adjacent parcels. 
I further certify that the data submitted herewith in support of this certification are 
accurate to the best of my knowledge, that the analyses have been performed correctly 
and in accordance with sound engineering practice, and that the proposed site 
development and structural works are designed in accordance with sound engineering 
practice.

NOTICE OF PLANNING DIRECTOR'S LAND USE DECISION 
MADE WITHOUT A HEARING 
Notice is hereby given that the Josephine Planning Director has made a land use decision regarding 
the following application: 
OWNER/APPLICANT: 
Dirling Revocable Living Trust /Gerlitz Engineering Consultants 
DECISION: 
Denial for the placement of Non-Farm Dwelling on the above-mentioned 
property located in the Exclusive Farm zone with access off Lower River Rd. 
LOCATION: 
Lower River Rd 
LEGAL: 
36-06-15-C0, Tax Lot (TL) 2800 
ZONE: 
Exclusive Farm (EF) 
PLANNER: 
Tami Smith (541) 474-5424; Email: tsmith@josephinecounty.gov 
INFORMATION: 
The application materials, as well as the applicable criteria, are available 
for review at the planning division upon request & please call 541-474- 
5421 for an appointment. Copies are available for a cost. 
Any person who is adversely affected, aggrieved or who is entitled to this 
notice according to law may appeal the Planning Director's decision to the 
Josephine County Board of County Commissioners. The appeal must be 
in writing using forms provided by the planning division. The fee is 
$250.00, and the appeal must satisfy all ordinance requirements. Land 
use regulations governing appeals are available at the planning division. 
The decision is not final until the appeal period expires without an appeal. 
The Board of County Commissioners decision may be appealed to LUBA. 
DEADLINE DA TE FOR 
APPEALS: 
Appeals to be submitted 'must be in writing' are due no later than 
5PM, June 15, 2026, on the 12th day from the postmark date. If the 
deadline falls on a weekend, the deadline shall be extended to the 
following Monday.

Josephine County, Oregon 
PLANNING DIRECTOR'S DECISION 
Non-Farm Dwelling 
SECTION 1. APPLICATION INFORMATION 
1.1 
Owner/Applicant: Dirling Revocable Living Trust 
1.2 
Representative: Gerlitz Engineering Consultants 
1.3 
Location: Lower River Rd./36-06-15-C0 TL 2800 
1.4 
Zoning: Exclusive Farm (EF) 
1.5 
Description of Request: Placement of a Non-Farm Dwelling on the above referenced 
property located in the Exclusive Farm zone with access off Lower River Rd. The site map 
is attached as Exhibit A. 
1.6 
Noticing Information: A pre-decision notice of the proposed non-farm dwelling was mailed 
on March 17, 2026, in conformance with the requirements of ORS 197.763 and Chapter 
19.32 of the Josephine County Code (JCC). Staff received one comment on the proposal 
within the comment period. Staff received a response from the representative on April 8, 
2026, addressing concerns. Staff received one comment outside the comment period on 
April 26, 2026. The notice list is attached as Exhibit B. 
1.7 
Decision: DENIAL 
SECTION 2. 
EVIDENCE 
2.1 
The evidence received both in favor of the request and in opposition is contained in the 
Director's file, along with all other documentation and evidence received and reviewed as 
part of this action. Other documentation may include aerial photographs, zoning maps, 
soil books, water studies, resource maps and inventories, facility plans, reports or 
comments from other agencies, and other like materials. These materials, in addition to 
the Director's file, are made a part of these findings by reference. 
SECTION 3. CAC NOTIFICATION AND RESPONSE 
3.1 
The property involved in this request is not located within the boundaries of a Citizens 
Advisory Committee (CAC). Comments or objections from the affected CAC, if any, have 
been considered in making this decision. 
SECTION 4. APPLICABLE REVIEW STANDARDS ANO CRITERIA AND FINDINGS OF 
FACT 
4.1 
Section 19.64.060 - General Requirements for New Dwellings 
A. 
The proposed non-farm dwelling will not force a significant change in or 
significantly increase the cost of accepted farm or forest practices on surrounding 
lands devoted to farm or forest use; 
· 
Dirling - Non-Farm Dwelling 
!&4§t1Ht A - 63 
within the study area contain dwellings, with an average parcel size of approximately 11. 13 
acres. This reflects an established pattern of rural residential development within the EF 
zone rather than large contiguous blocks devoted exclusively to commercial agricultural 
use. The applicant also evaluated the 57 undeveloped parcels within the study area to 
determine their potential eligibility for nonfarm dwellings. The evaluation considered 
predominant soil classifications, whether the parcels could reasonably be managed in 
conjunction with adjacent farm operations, the character of surrounding land uses, and 
the existing development pattem in the vicinity. Based on those factors, the analysis 
concluded that approximately six parcels within the study area may potentially qualify for 
nonfarm dwellings. Based on the evidence submitted, if the application had been 
approved, the proposed dwelling does not appear likely to materially alter the stability of 
the overall land use pattern within the surrounding area subject to OAR 660-033- 
0130(4)(c)(C). 
3. 
The dwelliog will be sited on ~ lot or parcel created before January 1, 1993, or on 
a lot or parcel created after January 1, 1993, when the lot or parcel was created in 
conformance with the requirements of Section JCC 19.64.090.C. 
Finding: Plannir,g and Assessor records indicate that the subject parcel (Tax Lot 2800) 
was originally created by Warranty Deed (V327 P786) on October 27, 1977. The parcel 
was subsequently reconfigured through Partition Plat 2025-31 on September 16, 2025, 
resulting in its current size of 2. 71 acres. Because the parcel was odginally created prior 
to January 1, 1993, and the subsequent reconfiguration was completed in conformance 
with applicable County land division requirements, If the application had been approved 
the parcel satisfies the requirements of this section. 
4. 
A non-farm dwelling may not be approved on a lot or parcel that is already 
established with a single-family dwelling is established on a lot or parcel as a lot of 
record dwelling per subsection 19.64.070.8, JCC. 
Finding: Planning and Assessor records indicate that the parcel is currently vacant, and 
no other dwelling exists on the property. 
5. 
Applicant shall submit proof that the lot or parcel has been disqualified for valuation 
for farm use under ORS 308A.050 to 308A.128 or other special assessment under 
ORS 308A.315, 321.257 to 321.390, 321. 700 to 321. 754 or 321.805 to 321.855. 
an.9 any additional tax imposed as the result of disqualification has been paid 
pursuant to the requirement of ORS 215.236. 
Finding: Per the Josephine County Assessor records the subject property is not receiving 
special farm assessment. 
4.3 
Section 19.64.080 - Siting Standards 
A. 
The dwelling or activities associated with the dwelling will not force a significant 
change in or significantly increase the cost of accepted farming or forest practices 
on nearby lands devoted to farm or forest use; 
Finding: If application had been approved it does not appear that the proposed 
development would materially interfere with accepted farm or forest practices in the area. 
Dirling - Non-Farm Dwelling 
~t A - 64 
8. 
The placement of dwellings shall be located on the least productive, buildable 
portion of the parcel taking into consideration terrain, adverse soil or land 
conditions, drainage and flooding, access, vegetation, location and the size of the 
tract; 
Finding: The applicant has submitted a no-rise certification prepared by a registered 
engineer in accordance with Chapter 19. 69A, JCC. Any change in horhesite location 
requires a new study. 
C. 
A lot or parcel shall not be considered unsuitable solely because of size or location 
if it can be reasonably be put to farm or forest use in conjunction with other land. 
Finding: The subject parcel is not considered unsuitable solely because of its size or 
location. Based on the mapped soil classifications, including the presence of Class II and 
other agriculturally capable soils, the parcel appears to be capable of being reasonably 
put to farm or forest use in conjunction with other land. 
D. 
If the parcel is under forest assessment, the dwelling shall be sited upon generally 
unsuitable land for the production of merchantable tree species recognized under 
the Forest Practice Rules considering terrain, adverse soil or land conditions, 
drainage and flooding, vegetation, location and size of the parcel; 
Finding: Per the Josephine County Assessor records the subject property is not receiving 
special forest assessment. 
E. 
The dwelling will not materially alter the stability of the overall land use pattern of 
the area; 
Finding: As discussed under Section 4.2.A(2) above, the dwelling does not appear to 
materially alter the stability of the overall land use pattern in the area. 
F. 
If the dwelling is established under JCC 19.64. 070.A or 8, then additional dwellings 
may not be approved or sited. 
Finding: No additional dwellings were proposed. 
4.4 
Section 19.69A. 060 - Administration in floodway areas 
The property contains the floodplain of the Rogue River. The proposed home-site is 
entirely within the floodway. 
The structure shall be located at the site which is least 
impacted by inundation. The dwelling shall meet all development permit requirements of 
Section 19.69A.080 JGC and all property development standards of Section 19.69A.090 
JGC. 
Finding: The applicant submitted a No-Rise Flood Analysis prepared by the Galli Group, 
which appears to demonstrate that the proposed development will not result in an increase 
in base flood elevation within the f/oodway. However, if the application had been approved, 
the applicant would have been required to submit an application for a Technical Plan 
Review to the Planning Department for floodplain review and verification of compliance 
with Sections 19. 69A. 080 and 19. 69A. 090, JGC. Any change in home site location requires 
a new study. 
Dirling - Non-Farm Dwelling 
fjfiiBit A - 65 
4.5 
Section 19.76.020.D - Wildfire and Emergency Safety Standards 
New dwellings in any zone other than the forest zone shall demonstrate compliance with 
all of the applicable standards of Chapter 19.76 JCC. 
Finding: If the application had been approved, the applicant would have been required to 
submit a wildfire safety plan demonstrating compliance with the requirements of Chapter 
19. 76, JCC. 
4.6 
Section 19.83.020 -Application of Standards (Erosion Control) 
These standards apply to any land use application including development and 
construction which would require any grading or filling on slopes that are 15% or greater 
or soils that are granitic in composition as mapped by the NRCS except when authorized 
or regulated by the Oregon Forest Practices Act. 
Finding: The property contains granitic soils. If the application had been approved, the 
applicant would have been required to submit an erosion and sediment control plan. 
4.7 
Public Comments: 
Staff received one comment within the comment period from Steve Rouse, Rogue 
Advocates, PO Box 624, Ashland, Oregon 97520. The comment stated that under 
Section 19.64.070.A.1, JCC, the applicant must demonstrate that the property is 
generally unsuitable for the production of farm crops, livestock, or merchantable 
tree species. The comment referenced NRCS soil classifications and noted that 
lands comprised predominantly of Class I-IV soils are generally presumed suitable 
for farm use. The comment further stated that land is not considered generally 
unsuitable solely because it is too small to farm independently if it can reasonably 
be managed in conjunction with adjacent commercial farm operations. The 
comment also. referenced the productive alluvial soils commonly associated with 
the Rogue River corridor and requested findings addressing compliance with 
Section 19.64.070.A.2, JCC, regarding impacts to the overall land use pattern, 
including consideration of floodplain, riparian, scenic, and surrounding farm and 
forest practices. In addition, the comment requested confirmation that the 
proposed dwelling is located on the least productive portion of the parcel and will 
not significantly interfere with accepted farm or forest practices. 
Staff also received one written comment outside the applicable comment period 
from Buck and Linda Baker, 4707 Lower River Road, Grants Pass, Oregon 97526. 
The comment stated that the they have resided on the adjacent property since 
1996 and have direct knowledge of the development history of the subject parcel 
has produced hay crops in the past and is bound by the Fort Vannoy Irrigation 
District services ditch for water purposes. The comment referenced Oregon 
Statewide Planning Goal 3 and expressed concerns regarding the protection of 
agricultural land, open space, scenic resources, and wildlife habitat from 
residential development. While there are some residential homes on large pieces 
of acreage, the homes typically are along the Rogue River or on Lower River Rd, 
not in the middle of a hayfield like subject property. There are multiple commercial 
agricultural operations that operate on even smaller parcels within the immediate 
area of subject parcel. The neighboring lot owned by the same owner has a home, 
Detached Living Space, and large recreational vehicle building. 
Dirling - Non-Farm Dwelling 
The applicant submitted a written response addressing concerns related to soil 
suitability and the "generally unsuitable" standard, cumulative impacts on the land 
use pattern, the sensitivity of the Rogue River corridor, farm use disqualification, 
siting of the dwelling on the least productive portion of the parcel, and potential 
impacts on surrounding farm and forest practices referring to the narrative 
submitted as part of the application addressing these concerns. 
Finding: Staff reviewed the submitted materials and addressed the identified 
issues in the findings above. 
SECTION 5. DECISION 
Based on the evidence and the findings of fact entered herein, the Planning Director finds that 
the application has not met the burden of proof. A proposed dwelling is not allowed if the tract 
upon which it would be sited already includes a dwelling, and the two other parcels in the tract 
contain residences. Therefore, the Planning Director hereby issues a decision of DENIAL for a 
forest dwelling on the subject property. 
Done and dated this 22nd day of May, 2026.
